# TCSlib uniformity audit

**Verdict: HOLD against the requested convention.** The HBC finding is confirmed, including its hybrid-model counterpart. Simulation-based non-malleability has a second simulator-uniformity gap. The scheme interfaces require uniform honest algorithms, and the asymptotic adversary/distinguisher quantifiers retain non-uniformity. No additional uniformity-based STRONGER security contract was found.

This is a read-only, static audit. No Lean process, build, repository edit, commit, PR update, or dispatch was performed. An admitted theorem is identified as admitted, not treated as a constructed simulator. The judgments concern uniformity, not a fresh correctness audit of all cryptographic proofs.

## Coverage

The complete baseline scope contains **433 Lean files**, with 2,902 definition/carrier declarations and 10,416 indexed declarations including lemmas. Every source file at each of the seven snapshots is hashed. All files differing from the baseline at a PR head were separately indexed, including branch-age differences: **3,140 definition/carrier variants** in total. Unchanged definitions reuse the baseline component classification. The evidence includes both each PR's patch against its merge base and its complete file-difference list against the requested baseline.

The table and appendix contain **1,160 component rows**: 1,144 OK, 12 WEAKER, 4 EQUIVALENT-BY-AUX, and 0 STRONGER. The WEAKER rows are occurrences of the simulator issues U-01–U-03, not twelve unrelated defects. The definition and file coverage indexes include derived functions, encodings and mathematical carriers that have no separate machine-security quantifier; the finite-kernel appendix deliberately includes function-valued operands as well as literal machine types.


## How to read the component tables

`Uniform` means exactly empty advice at every parameter (`PPT.lean:293`). `PPT` is that subtype. `PolyTimeComputable` supplies one deterministic machine, and `Strategy.Efficient` supplies one uniform `NUPPT` realizing the entire next-message function. A `noncomputable def` choosing one such machine from a proof does not itself introduce per-length advice. Conversely, polynomial-time `A.output` does not make `A.runAt` uniform: `runAt` supplies `A.advice n`.

The contract table below classifies each security quantifier and each bundled computational component separately. Appendix A lists individual machine operands, machine-producing definitions, raw strategies, and finite-kernel components. The CSV has the same rows. Repeated uses of a scheme inherit its field contracts; arbitrary experiment arguments do not constitute a new claim of security or implementability.

There are two essential scope distinctions. Ordinary `InteractiveProof` intentionally permits an unbounded prover, as does PS10 Definition 116.1; `EfficientProverProof` is the interface for a uniform efficient honest prover. Also, a finite probability kernel, algebraic simulator, target functionality, or arbitrary ensemble is not a claim of a PPT implementation. Such rows state the missing computational obligation explicitly. Applying the requested convention to a deployed cryptographic protocol means using its efficient-party/implementation contract, not assuming that its raw data carrier proves efficiency.

**WEAKER/STRONGER refer to the security contract, accounting for quantifier polarity.** The brief's example of a uniform-only adversary under `∀` reverses this polarity: restricting a universally quantified adversary makes security easier to satisfy, hence WEAKER. No repair is proposed merely because a raw kernel accepts more general mathematical functions.

## Findings and repairs

### U-01 — Major — Both HBC security predicates omit simulator uniformity

At baseline:

```lean
-- SecureComputation/Defs.lean:176
F.PolyTime π ∧ ∃ SA SB : NUPPT, ∀ D : NUPPT, ...

-- SecureComputation/Hybrid.lean:259
H.PolyTime F ∧ ∃ SA SB : NUPPT, ∀ D : NUPPT, ...
```

Neither existential includes `SA.Uniform` or `SB.Uniform`. The honest strategies are uniform through `PolyTime`, and `D` correctly remains non-uniform. The simulator choices are made before `D`, but that quantifier order does not eliminate their per-length advice. The same weak interfaces are present at all six PR heads. `yaoHybrid_simulators` also states only a bare simulator existential.

**Repair:** strengthen both predicates, their readers, and the Yao simulator statement to the following shape, or use `PPT` witnesses:

```lean
... ∧ ∃ SA SB : NUPPT, SA.Uniform ∧ SB.Uniform ∧
  ∀ D : NUPPT, ...
```

Keep the negligible bound before all inputs, and keep `D : NUPPT`. OT can supply its existing `otSimA_uniform` and `otSimB_uniform` proofs. Composition needs the uniform construction described in U-03. The currently admitted Yao simulator statement must acquire actual uniform witnesses; its prose sketch is insufficient evidence of a completed proof.

**Source correction:** this is a gap against the user's convention and Goldreich's uniform-simulation approach. It is **not** a discrepancy with PS10 Definition 155.1: the original PDF, book pp. 155–156, explicitly permits “a pair of n.u. p.p.t. simulator algorithms”. The brief's PS10 attribution of the gap is refuted. Goldreich's author-hosted precursor explicitly requires uniform simulation in the discussion on p. 19. The precise published Gol04 edition was not separately text-extracted; the convention judgment does not depend on that edition comparison. See U-05 and the source list.

Every existing declaration at the baseline whose interface or constructor/destructor proof is affected by this repair is listed here. The two raw `idealA`/`idealB` experiment functions can remain general.

| Declaration (all under `TCSlib.Cryptography.SecureComputation`) | Baseline file:line | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `HBCSecure` | `SecureComputation/Defs.lean:176` | Strengthen simulator contract. |
| `hbcSecure_iff` | `SecureComputation/Defs.lean:184` | Expose both new uniformity clauses. |
| `HybridProtocol.HBCSecure` | `SecureComputation/Hybrid.lean:259` | Strengthen hybrid simulator contract. |
| `HybridProtocol.hbcSecure_iff` | `SecureComputation/Hybrid.lean:267` | Expose both new clauses. |
| `otProtocol_hbcSecure` | `SecureComputation/ObliviousTransfer.lean:663` | Repackage the explicit, already uniform OT witnesses. |
| `yaoHybrid_simulators` | `SecureComputation/Yao.lean:907` | Strengthen its conclusion; proof remains admitted in audited snapshots. |
| `yaoHybrid_hbcSecure` | `SecureComputation/Yao.lean:931` | Repackage strengthened simulator witnesses. |
| `HybridProtocol.hbcSecure_compose` | `SecureComputation/ComposeSecurity.lean:375` | Semantics strengthen with both hypotheses and conclusion; baseline proof is admitted. |
| `yaoProtocol_hbcSecure` | `SecureComputation/YaoReal.lean:95` | Transitive consumer of OT, hybrid Yao and composition; statement inherits the strengthened predicate. |

PR #318's `otProtocol_hbcSecure` is at line 740; its explicit simulators already suffice. PR #323's Yao simulator and HBC wrapper are at lines 935 and 959; both require the same migration. PR #324 adds `HybridProtocol.exists_simA` (`SideA.lean:381`), `exists_simB` (`SideB.lean:366`), and `ComposeSim.exists_simulator` (`Sim.lean:236`); these are additional affected consumers, detailed in U-03. Its final composition theorem is at `ComposeSecurity.lean:429`. The other three PRs add no HBC consumer. Static searches across all of `TCSlib`, not just the two audit directories, found no additional consumer of the affected security predicates or their readers. Per-snapshot matches are retained in `evidence/direct-consumers.json`.

### U-02 — Major — `SimNMSecure` permits simulator advice, and adding `Uniform` alone is untenable

`Encryption/PublicKey/NonMalleability/Simulation.lean:672` has

```lean
∀ (M : MsgSampler) (A : NUPPT) (q r sl : PolyExpr),
  ∃ Sim : NUPPT, ∀ D : NUPPT, ...
```

Its `MsgSampler.samp` is correctly adversary-controlled and non-uniform; `hist` is uniformly polynomial-time. The missing requirement is a **uniform simulator program with access to the adversary's existing advice as shared auxiliary information**, rather than an independent simulator advice family.

The current CPA construction actually uses advice. In `Simulation/Simulator.lean:608`, `NMSim.exists_simulator` first applies `exists_interactiveHandlerMachine A ...`, whose machine carries `A.advice n` and the handler's bounded initial state (`Games/Efficiency.lean:847`), then applies `NUPPT.exists_withAdviceOn` with `samp.advice`. Thus it carries both adversarial advice families. `PolyTimeComputable simMsg` proves only the raw function efficient when these bits are supplied; it does not compute them.

**Why a one-line repair fails.** This is a mathematical argument, not a Lean-checked counterexample:

1. Choose an undecidable bit sequence `b : ℕ → Bool`. Choose a uniform constant message sampler with empty history; set the real adversary's query budget and ciphertext-output count to zero and its auxiliary-output length `sl` to one.
2. A non-uniform PPT adversary can output a legal return with `σ = [b(n)]`, using that single advice bit. A uniform distinguisher reads this bit from the experiment's encoded view.
3. If a uniform ideal simulator receiving only the public key satisfied the modified definition, let `p(n)` be the probability that the ideal experiment's `σ` bit is true. The keys, sampler, simulator and empty history oracle would all be uniform, so `p(n)` is a computable rational: enumerate their finite coin spaces.
4. Security would imply

   \[
   |\mathbf 1_{b(n)=\mathrm{true}}-p(n)|\leq\varepsilon(n),\qquad
   \varepsilon(n)\longrightarrow0.
   \]

   Consequently, for all sufficiently large `n`,

   \[
   b(n)=\mathrm{true}\iff p(n)>\tfrac12.
   \]

   Computing `p(n)` and hard-coding the finitely many exceptions decides `b`, a contradiction.

The original paper's PPT-program formulation and this repository's explicitly non-uniform adversary adaptation must therefore be distinguished; simply combining the latter with an advice-free, auxiliary-input-free simulator is not an implementation repair.

**Repair preserving non-uniform adversaries:** add an ideal experiment whose **ordinary input** to a uniform simulator contains the public key and shared auxiliary data, including `A.advice n` and `M.samp.advice n`. These inputs have polynomial length bounded by the existing advice polynomials and the pairing overhead. Require `Sim.Uniform`; allow no additional private simulator advice. Equivalently formulate the adversary and sampler as fixed uniform programs with arbitrary polynomially bounded shared auxiliary strings. Do not make the adversary or adversary-chosen sampler uniform without auxiliary input.

A migration can preserve the existing raw `snmIdeal` and its helpers, add `snmIdealAux` and its transport lemmas, and change the security predicate to use the new experiment. Under that conservative repair, the complete existing statement/proof consumers at the tip are:

| Declaration (under `Composability.FixedLengthPKScheme`) | File:line | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `SimNMSecure` | `Simulation.lean:672` | Uniform simulator and explicit shared auxiliary input. |
| `SimNMSecure_iff` | `Simulation.lean:681` | New contract reader. |
| `nmSecure_of_simNMSecure` | `Simulation.lean:720` | Use the auxiliary-input ideal-gap argument. |
| `simNMSecure_of_nmSecure_cpa` | `Simulation.lean:771` | Build and certify a uniform simulator supplied with shared advice. |
| `simNMSecure_cpa_iff_nmSecure_cpa` | `Simulation.lean:792` | Reassemble the migrated implications. |

All paths in this table are under `TCSlib/Cryptography/Encryption/PublicKey/NonMalleability/`. No other declaration refers to `SimNMSecure` at any audited head. The current machinery needing **new uniform/auxiliary-input counterparts**, rather than an unsupported reuse as a uniformity certificate, is `NMSim.exists_simulator` (`Simulation/Simulator.lean:608`) and the ideal-law/gap transport (`NMSim.idealAcc`, `ideal_gap`, and `FixedLengthPKScheme.accept_snmIdeal_eq`). The general non-uniform handler and `exists_withAdviceOn` theorems remain valid and need not be changed. None of the six PRs changes this interface or supplies this repair.

### U-03 — Minor — The composition proof does not yet produce `Uniform` witnesses

At PR #324, the exact simulator construction is:

```text
HybridProtocol.exists_simA / exists_simB
  → ComposeSim.exists_simulator
  → ComposeSim.exists_post
  → NUPPT.ofPolyTime
```

`Sim.lean:236–242` post-processes the hybrid simulator and supplies the subprotocol simulator's advice. The witness in `Reductions.lean:100` has

\[
S.\mathrm{advice}(n)
=\operatorname{pairEncode}(SH.\mathrm{advice}(n),S\rho.\mathrm{advice}(n)).
\]

The current `SH` and `Sρ` are obtained from the weak HBC predicates, so they need not be uniform. Even after strengthening those hypotheses, this **particular witness** still has paired empty advice. By the encoding's length formula,

\[
|\operatorname{pairEncode}([],[])|=2\cdot0+2+0=2\ne0.
\]

It therefore fails the repository's literal `NUPPT.Uniform`, despite then having a uniform behavior. This is an encoding/closure issue, not evidence of an essential need for advice.

**Repair:** add a uniform post-processing constructor with uniform `SH` and `Sρ` hypotheses. Its raw polynomial-time function inserts the known empty slots itself, and its outer machine uses `NUPPT.ofPolyTimeUniform`. Strengthen `ComposeSim.exists_simulator` and `HybridProtocol.exists_simA`/`exists_simB` to return the uniformity proofs; pass them through `hbcSecure_compose`. The existing general non-uniform `exists_post` can remain unchanged. The complete affected existing consumer chain is those four declarations, the final `hbcSecure_compose`, and transitive `yaoProtocol_hbcSecure`; the exact locations are in U-01 and the matrix.

**The worst-instance advice does not reach a simulator.** `Template.lean:158` chooses the bad-instance template for a reduction; `exists_gap_le` applies `exists_pre` to build the distinguisher `Dh`. `Correct.lean` similarly chooses output-comparison advice for distinguishers. `exists_dPhi` is another distinguisher, allowed to carry advice. The simulators above depend only on `SH`, `Sρ`, the uniform call-input evaluator and protocol parameters. In `SideA.lean:389` / `SideB.lean:374` they are chosen **before** `D` and before the bad-instance choices. Thus step 3's advice is correctly confined to reductions. Removing it is neither necessary nor desirable.

### U-04 — Note — ZK's auxiliary-input bound is adequate

`ZK` uses `V'.Efficient`, but it is **EQUIVALENT-BY-AUX**, not a uniform-only adversary restriction. For any polynomial-length advice family `α(n)`, choose a polynomial majorant `Lz`; all words `z = α(|x|)` are covered. More generally, pairing advice and an auxiliary word has length

\[
|\operatorname{pairEncode}(a,z)|=2|a|+2+|z|,
\]

so a polynomial bound remains a polynomial bound. `PolyExpr` includes constants and powers of `n+1`, which suffice to majorize every polynomial advice bound, including `n=0`. A fixed uniform interpreter can read the advice as auxiliary data; the simulator gets the **same** auxiliary data. Its choice occurs before `Lz` and `D`, so a separate simulator is not chosen for each advice string or length bound.

`HVZK` has the same `∀ Lz` bound. `StatZK`, `PerfectZK`, and `PerfectZKAbort` quantify over all `z` without a bound. The non-uniform distinguishers remain bare `NUPPT`. This checks the uniformity/auxiliary-input convention; it is not a new Lean proof of a general strategy-interpreter equivalence. PS10 explicitly explains this auxiliary-input treatment in the paragraph following Definition 121.2.

PR #327 preserves these definitions and constructs `SQ.Uniform` explicitly with `NUPPT.ofPolyTimeUniform` (`SequentialZK/SimulatorMachine.lean:310–344`). Its selected bad-prefix/advice data belongs to computational reductions, not to `SQ`'s advice. The proof uses the original simulator's `Uniform` assumption in its prefix/suffix implementations.

The disclosed strict-PPT versus expected-PPT simulator distinction is a separate running-time-model issue; the uniformity audit does not claim those two time models are identical.

### U-05 — Minor — Correct the brief's source and polarity claims

The brief's statement that HBC simulator advice is weaker than PS10 §6.2 is false: Definition 155.1 explicitly permits it. Describe U-01 as migration to the user's convention, with the Goldreich comparison kept distinct. Also correct its `∀`-adversary example as explained before the matrix. These are audit-specification corrections, not changes to Lean theorem bodies. Their downstream impact is the uniformity audit README/brief and any future audit or migration rationale repeating those assertions; no cryptographic declaration needs a change solely to make the PS10 citation accurate.

### U-06 — Note — Parameter and auxiliary information reach the asymptotic adversaries

The ordinary asymptotic games use `runAt`/`runAtL` at the security parameter; their machine layout includes `advInput n y = ⟨1^n,y⟩`. The oracle adapters keep `n`, advice and the random tape through repeated activations. Thus CPA/CCA, signatures, MACs, collision finding, DDH, LWE, SIS, PRF oracle tests, learning and HBC distinguishers do not become uniform or lose the security parameter merely because the visible game argument is a public key, ciphertext or transcript. Polynomial-length auxiliary information can be put in their unrestricted advice. PR #326's bad-input advice is in a PRG distinguisher; its deterministic seed-enumeration host is one fixed machine.

Two explicitly named literal variants need to remain distinct:

| Definition | Information supplied | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| `Ensemble.Indist` | `1^n`, sample and advice at `n` | OK. |
| `Ensemble.IndistLit` | sample and advice at its output length | Non-uniform; named literal convention. For nonconstant `PolyExpr` output length, the existing injectivity bridge recovers `n` by advice. For constant width, finitely many point tests suffice for indistinguishability; that constant-case bridge is not formalized. |
| `FunFamily.WorstCaseOneWay` | `1^n` and image | Headline parameterized hardness notion; OK. |
| `FunFamily.WorstCaseOneWayLit` | image and advice at image length | Non-uniform but can genuinely know less when output length is constant. The code explicitly documents this and requires nonconstant/injective output length for its reverse bridge. It is not a substitute for the headline notion. |

The last limitation is already disclosed: a constant-output family with growing input length can look hard to the literal inverter because it cannot know the required preimage length. Its only consumers are the `WorstCaseOneWay.toLit` and `WorstCaseOneWayLit.toParam` bridges. No scheme's security in this audit is based on silently substituting that literal notion. If a uniform project-wide `1^n` convention is desired even for literal variants, retain them as clearly named historical comparisons or require nonconstant output length, rather than claiming unconditional equivalence.

### U-07 — Note — Abstract laws and finite algebra are not efficient constructions

`Ensemble`, `OracleEnsemble`, LWE `Noise` and the LWE/SIS numeric parameter families describe mathematical environments. They do not certify efficient generation. The implementation-facing obligations are separate (`Samplable`, `_uniform`, `PolyTimeComputable`, or a construction theorem). `effSources` has a uniform sampler witness at each fixed `n`; it does not assert a single sampler realizing an arbitrarily prescribed entire source family. Its impossibility theorem is explicitly pointwise in `n`.

Similarly, secret sharing, the BGW algebraic kernel, the finite OWF/PRF probability layer, `MultiEnc`, and Schnorr's algebraic `simulate`/`extract` do not bundle PPT machines. Their correctness/statistical claims can be checked without pretending that an arbitrary abstract field or domain has an efficient representation. The matrix records this, rather than marking unrestricted finite data as a non-uniform honest algorithm. A use claiming uniform PPT must add the corresponding encoding and complexity obligations.

| Baseline `otProtocol_hbcSecure` | **Its chosen witnesses are uniform.** | Packages the two explicit machines; its security dependency `otProtocol_simulators` is still admitted at this baseline. Adding the two existing uniformity proofs fixes the packaging. |
| PR #318 `otProtocol_simulators` / `otProtocol_hbcSecure` | **Yes.** | Explicit machines at 492 and 604; uniformity proofs at 502 and 615; security assembly at 700 and 740. The proof's `viewDist` and signed hardcore predictors are non-uniform reductions, not simulators. |
| Baseline `HybridProtocol.hbcSecure_compose` | **No constructed witnesses to assess.** | `ComposeSecurity.lean:375` is admitted. The sketch alone does not certify uniformity. |
| PR #324 `ComposeSim.exists_simulator` | **No, in general; and not literally `Uniform` even on empty component advice.** | `Sim.lean:236`; paired advice in `Reductions.lean:100`. U-03 supplies the repair. |
| PR #324 `HybridProtocol.exists_simA` | **No guarantee.** | Extracts potentially non-uniform `SH`, `SρA`; uses the above closure at `SideA.lean:389`. No bad-instance reduction advice is used. |
| PR #324 `HybridProtocol.exists_simB` | **No guarantee.** | Symmetric construction at `SideB.lean:374`. |

| `yaoHybrid_hbcSecure` / `yaoProtocol_hbcSecure` across snapshots | **Delegated, not new constructions.** | Use the admitted Yao existential and the relevant composition theorem; cannot establish uniformity from their current types. |
| PR #319 | **No `SA`/`SB` construction.** | Deterministic canonizer work; it supplies no simulator-uniformity repair. |
| PR #323 apart from Yao's admitted statement | **No new `SA`/`SB` construction.** | Special-encryption/garbling machines are uniform honest constructions; slice advice is in adversarial reductions. |
| PR #326 | **No `SA`/`SB` construction.** | Uniform deterministic host; bad-input advice is only in a PRG distinguisher. |
| PR #327 `exists_phaseSimulator` (ZK `SQ`, not HBC `SA`/`SB`) | **Yes.** | Explicit `SQ.Uniform` and `ofPolyTimeUniform`; its phase simulator is also required uniform. |
| Baseline `NMSim.exists_simulator` (not an HBC simulator) | **No.** | Carries adversary and sampler advice; U-02. |

## PR-specific disposition

| PR | Uniformity result |
|---|---|
| #318 | Honest parties' efficiency proof is uniform; both OT simulators are already uniform. Its HBC wrapper needs only the U-01 contract migration. |
| #319 | Deterministic code/canonizer constructions, with no new non-uniform honest machine or simulator. |
| #323 | Honest special-encryption constructions retain `_uniform`; slice/worst-instance advice is in distinguishers. Yao simulation is still admitted and has the weak existential. |
| #324 | Parties are proved uniform through `Strategy.Efficient`. Simulator uniformity is not supplied; strengthen both input security contracts and repair paired advice as U-03. |
| #326 | The PRG distinguisher is intentionally non-uniform; the generated deterministic decider is uniform. No simulator issue. |
| #327 | Lifted verifiers use auxiliary data; phase and repeated simulators are explicitly uniform. No new uniformity defect. |

## Evidence and limits

The bundle contains the verified exchange instructions, immutable PR heads, source-file hashes for every audited snapshot, full declaration extracts, the component matrix in CSV/JSON, definition/file coverage, direct-consumer matches, the six PR diffs, and the read-only extraction scripts. The index is comment-aware textual extraction, **not Lean elaboration or an exact compiled dependency graph**. Consumer claims above were checked at their concrete use sites and by whole-`TCSlib` searches; abstract new-helper names in the proposed repairs are design suggestions, not checked Lean code. Nothing in this report supplies the missing admitted Yao proof or claims a kernel-checked repair.

## Sources

- **PS10:** R. Pass and a. shelat, [A Course in Cryptography, January 2010](https://www.cs.cornell.edu/courses/cs4830/2010fa/lecnotes.pdf). Checked §2.1's computational conventions; Definitions 121.1–121.2 and the following auxiliary-input explanation; Definition 155.1; §7.1.6; §8.3. Definition numbers in the matrix refer to this text.
- **Gol04:** O. Goldreich, [Foundations of Cryptography, Volume 2](https://www.wisdom.weizmann.ac.il/~oded/foc-vol2.html), §§7.2–7.3 as cited by the repository. Independently accessible primary corroboration of uniform simulation: the author's [Secure Multi-Party Computation, version 1.4](https://www.wisdom.weizmann.ac.il/~oded/PSX/prot.pdf), pp. 16–19, especially the explicit uniformity observation on p. 19. This precursor is not represented as the published 2004 edition.
- **Gol01:** O. Goldreich, [Foundations of Cryptography, Chapter 4 draft](https://www.wisdom.weizmann.ac.il/~oded/PSBookFrag/part4N.pdf), §4.3 and sequential/auxiliary-input zero knowledge.
- **PSV07:** R. Pass, a. shelat and V. Vaikuntanathan, [Relations Among Notions of Non-Malleability for Encryption](https://www.cs.cornell.edu/~rafael/papers/newdefs.pdf), Definition 1. Its sampler/program model is distinguished from this repository's non-uniform adaptation.
- **AB09:** S. Arora and B. Barak, *Computational Complexity: A Modern Approach*, Chapter 7; [author-hosted draft](https://theory.cs.princeton.edu/complexity/book.pdf). Used for the uniform randomized-machine versus non-uniform circuit distinction.
- **Pei16:** C. Peikert, [A Decade of Lattice Cryptography](https://web.eecs.umich.edu/~cpeikert/pubs/lattice-survey.pdf), §2.4 and Definitions 4.1.1, 4.2.1–4.2.3. The source gives the security parameter to every algorithm and explicitly allows stronger non-uniform adversaries. This audit checks the Lean uniformity contracts, not every lattice parameter or hardness reduction.
- **Gar26 / finite algebra sources:** the repository's source labels identify the finite games and algebraic protocols. The judgments here distinguish those mathematical kernels from asymptotic implementations; no independent source-wide correctness audit of those layers is claimed.

## Glossary

- `SA`, `SB`: Alice's and Bob's HBC simulators, as in the audited code.
- `SH`, `Sρ`: the hybrid-protocol and subprotocol simulators used by composition.
- `SQ`: the repeated ZK simulator constructed by PR #327.
- `α(n)`: a polynomial-length advice family; `Lz`: its polynomial auxiliary-input bound.
- `b(n)`, `p(n)`: the undecidable advice bit and the ideal experiment's computable acceptance probability in U-02.
- `σ`: the non-malleability experiment's auxiliary output; `ε(n)`: a negligible error bound.
